What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I’m Ruben. I guess I’m here because lately, most days when I wake up, there’s this heavy, kind of hollow feeling in my chest. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it feels like the old me—the guy who could talk to anybody and actually enjoy the day—just isn’t really there right now. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, Ruben?\n\nPatient: I’d say… about eight months, give or take. It kind of started after the new regional manager came in and started, um, really leaning on me—taking away some accounts I’d had for years. I used to look forward to bowling, messing around in the garden, taking my wife to little gallery shows, even going dancing sometimes. Now I kind of just… sit there, or I make some excuse not to go. I don’t trust myself the way I used to, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you been experiencing any excessive worry or fear about everyday things or situations during this period?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not excessive, anyway. I don’t like saying that because it sounds kind of dramatic, but I keep telling myself I’m useless now. I try not to make it sound worse than it is, but my mind goes to, you know, “what good am I?” Like I keep telling myself I’m useless now, and then it feels like the years I spent taking care of clients and providing for my family don’t count for much. I try not to make it sound worse than it is, but my mind goes to, you know, “what good am I?” Like I keep telling myself I’m useless now, and then it feels like the years I spent taking care of clients and providing for my family don’t count for much. I know that probably isn’t a fair way to look at it… but that’s where I end up.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed myself moving slower, exactly… and I don’t think I’m pacing around or anything like that. Maybe I’m just not paying close enough attention. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think about it better.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sitting still more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say that, exactly. I mean… I sit down when I’m tired like anyone else, but I haven’t really noticed if it’s more than before. Maybe I should pay more attention to it. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been fidgeting or having trouble staying still more often than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I’ve really noticed. I don’t like saying that because it sounds kind of dramatic, but I keep telling myself I’m useless now. Like all those years of taking care of clients and providing for my family somehow don’t count, because I can’t even handle this job anymore. I know maybe that’s not fair to say about myself, but… it’s where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself critical of yourself, beyond just this current situation at work?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, I can be pretty hard on myself, not just about work. I try not to make it sound worse than it is, but my mind goes to, you know, “what good am I?” Like I keep telling myself I’m useless now, and then it feels like the years I spent taking care of clients and providing for my family don’t count for much. I know that probably isn’t a fair way to look at it… but that’s where I end up.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed myself moving slower, exactly… and I don’t think I’m pacing around or anything like that. Maybe I’m just not paying close enough attention. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think about it better.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sitting still more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say that, exactly. I mean… I sit down when I’m tired like anyone else, but I haven’t really noticed if it’s more than before. Maybe I should pay more attention to it. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been fidgeting or having trouble staying still more often than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I’ve really noticed. I’m not usually tapping my foot or getting up and down a lot, at least I don’t think I am. I might shift around some, but nothing that stood out to me as more than usual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than you used to or having trouble doing physical activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, no. I mean, I’m not doing anything too physical most days, so it’s kind of hard for me to judge. I haven’t really noticed myself being slower in a clear way. If other people have noticed, they haven’t said anything. Is that helpful?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I’m Ruben. I guess I’m here because lately, most days when I wake up, there’s this heavy, kind of hollow feeling in my chest. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it feels like the old me—the guy who could talk to anybody and actually enjoy the day—just isn’t really there right now. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, Ruben?\n\nPatient: I’d say… about eight months, give or take. It kind of started after the new regional manager came in and started, um, really leaning on me—taking away some accounts I’d had for years. I tried to tell myself it was just work stuff and I’d adjust, but… it’s been hanging around since then. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Ruben, do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like before this period started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I hate to say it, but not really. I used to look forward to bowling, messing around in the garden, taking my wife to little gallery shows, even going dancing sometimes. Now I kind of just… sit there, or I make some excuse not to go. It all feels like more effort than it’s worth, which I know probably sounds bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, or has that changed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s changed. I mean, I can still go through the motions sometimes, but it doesn’t feel the same. I used to really look forward to bowling, working in the garden, taking my wife to gallery shows, even going dancing once in a while. Now I mostly just sit there, or I come up with excuses not to go, because none of it feels like it’s worth the effort. I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you were dead during this period?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want to alarm anyone, and I haven’t really wanted to say it out loud. But yes, I’ve had thoughts like… maybe my wife and kids would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And that scares me. I don’t trust myself the way I used to, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you been experiencing any excessive worry or fear about everyday things or situations during this period?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not excessive, anyway. I mean, I worry about normal things, like anybody would, but I wouldn’t say I’m afraid of everyday situations or anything like that. Unless I’m misunderstanding what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or the way you move, like feeling slowed down or restless?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything like that, exactly. I mean, I’m probably not as… lively as I used to be, but I don’t know if I’d call it being slowed down or restless. Maybe my wife would notice more than I do. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you noticed any significant changes in your weight during this period?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in any big way. My clothes seem about the same, and I haven’t really been checking the scale much. If there’s been a change, I don’t think it’s been significant… is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Ruben, do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, more than I probably should. I don’t like saying that because it sounds kind of dramatic, but I keep telling myself I’m useless now. Like all those years of taking care of clients and providing for my family somehow don’t count, because I can’t even handle this job anymore. I know maybe that’s not fair to say about myself, but… it’s where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself critical of yourself, beyond just this current situation at work?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, I can be pretty hard on myself, not just about work. I try not to make it sound worse than it is, but my mind goes to, you know, “what good am I?” Like I keep telling myself I’m useless now, and then it feels like the years I spent taking care of clients and providing for my family don’t count for much. I know that probably isn’t a fair way to look at it… but that’s where I end up.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed myself moving slower, exactly… and I don’t think I’m pacing around or anything like that. Maybe I’m just not paying close enough attention. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been fidgeting or having trouble staying still more often than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I’ve really noticed. I’m not usually tapping my foot or getting up and down a lot, at least I don’t think I am. I might shift around some, but nothing that stood out to me as more than usual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than you used to or having trouble doing physical activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, no. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },

I tried to tell myself it was just work stuff and I’d adjust, but… it’s been hanging around since then. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Ruben, do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like before this period started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I hate to say it, but not really. I used to look forward to bowling, messing around in the garden, taking my wife to little gallery shows, even going dancing sometimes. Now I kind of just… sit there, or I make some excuse not to go. It all feels like more effort than it’s worth, which I know probably sounds bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, or has that changed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s changed. I mean, I can still go through the motions sometimes, but it doesn’t feel the same. I used to really look forward to bowling, working in the garden, taking my wife to gallery shows, even going dancing once in a while. Now I mostly just sit there, or I come up with excuses not to go, because none of it feels like it’s worth the effort. I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you were dead during this period?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want to alarm anyone, and I haven’t really wanted to say it out loud. But yes, I’ve had thoughts like… maybe my wife and kids would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And that scares me. I don’t trust myself the way I used to, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you been experiencing any excessive worry or fear about everyday things or situations during this period?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not excessive, anyway. I mean, I worry about normal things, like anybody would, but I wouldn’t say I’m afraid of everyday situations or anything like that. Unless I’m misunderstanding what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or the way you move, like feeling slowed down or restless?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything like that, exactly. I mean, I’m probably not as… lively as I used to be, but I don’t know if I’d call it being slowed down or restless. Maybe my wife would notice more than I do. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you noticed any significant changes in your weight during this period?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in any big way. My clothes seem about the same, and I haven’t really been checking the scale much. If there’s been a change, I don’t think it’s been significant… is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Ruben, do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, more than I probably should. I don’t like saying that because it sounds kind of dramatic, but I keep telling myself I’m useless now. Like all those years of taking care of clients and providing for my family somehow don’t count, because I can’t even handle this job anymore. I know maybe that’s not fair to say about myself, but… it’s where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself critical of yourself, beyond just this current situation at work?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, I can be pretty hard on myself, not just about work. I try not to make it sound worse than it is, but my mind goes to, you know, “what good am I?” Like I keep telling myself I’m useless now, and then it feels like the years I spent taking care of clients and providing for my family don’t count for much. I know that probably isn’t a fair way to look at it… but that’s where I end up.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed myself moving slower, exactly… and I don’t think I’m pacing around or anything like that. Maybe I’m just not paying close enough attention. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think about it better.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sitting still more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say that, exactly. I mean… I sit down when I’m tired like anyone else, but I haven’t really noticed if it’s more than before. Maybe I should pay more attention to it. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been fidgeting or having trouble staying still more often than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I’ve really noticed. I’m not usually tapping my foot or getting up and down a lot, at least I don’t think I am. I might shift around some, but nothing that stood out to me as more than usual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than you used to or having trouble doing physical activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, no. I mean, I’m not doing anything too physical most days, so it’s kind of hard for me to judge. I haven’t really noticed myself being slower in a clear way. If other people have noticed, they haven’t said anything. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having trouble concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure. I mean, I guess everybody has moments where they lose focus or put off a decision, right? I haven’t noticed anything that feels obviously worse than usual, at least not enough that I’d want to make too big a deal of it. But maybe I’m not the best judge. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed a big weight loss, no. My clothes don’t feel dramatically different or anything like that. I mean, I don’t weigh myself all the time, so I might not be the best person to say for sure, but nothing significant that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having trouble staying still or feeling restless?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in any obvious way. I mean, I might shift around or get up sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I feel like I can’t sit still or anything like that. Nobody’s mentioned me seeming restless, at least. It kind of started around when the new regional manager came in and started really coming down on me, taking away accounts I’d handled for years. I don’t want to sound like I’m blaming everything on work, but that’s about when things stopped feeling worth the effort.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, would you say that your depressive feelings have been consistently worse over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days lately I wake up with this heavy, kind of hollow feeling in my chest. Like the old Ruben—the guy who could talk to anybody and actually enjoy his day—just isn’t really there. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, I’m probably not as… lively as I used to be, but I don’t know if I’d call it being slowed down or restless. Maybe my wife would notice more than I do. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Ruben, have you noticed any significant changes in your weight during this period?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in any big way. My clothes seem about the same, and I haven’t really been checking the scale much. If there’s been a change, I don’t think it’s been significant… is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Ruben, do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, more than I probably should. I don’t like saying that because it sounds kind of dramatic, but I keep telling myself I’m useless now. Like all those years of taking care of clients and providing for my family somehow don’t count, because I can’t even handle this job anymore. I know maybe that’s not fair to say about myself, but… it’s where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself critical of yourself, beyond just this current situation at work?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, I can be pretty hard on myself, not just about work. I try not to make it sound worse than it is, but my mind goes to, you know, “what good am I?” Like I keep telling myself I’m useless now, and then it feels like the years I spent taking care of clients and providing for my family don’t count for much. I know that probably isn’t a fair way to look at it… but that’s where I end up.\n\nDoctor: Ruben, do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed myself moving slower, exactly… and I don’t think I’m pacing around or anything like that. Maybe I’m just not paying close enough attention. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to think about it better.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sitting still more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say that, exactly. I mean… I sit down when I’m tired like anyone else, but I haven’t really noticed if it’s more than before. Maybe I should pay more attention to it. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been fidgeting or having trouble staying still more often than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I’ve really noticed. I’m not usually tapping my foot or getting up and down a lot, at least I don’t think I am. I might shift around some, but nothing that stood out to me as more than usual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than you used to or having trouble doing physical activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, no. I mean, I’m not doing anything too physical most days, so it’s kind of hard for me to judge. I haven’t really noticed myself being slower in a clear way. If other people have noticed, they haven’t said anything. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having trouble concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure. I mean, I guess everybody has moments where they lose focus or put off a decision, right? I haven’t noticed anything that feels obviously worse than usual, at least not enough that I’d want to make too big a deal of it. But maybe I’m not the best judge. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed a big weight loss, no. My clothes don’t feel dramatically different or anything like that. I mean, I might shift around or get up sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I feel like I can’t sit still or anything like that. Nobody’s mentioned me seeming restless, at least. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like activities you used to enjoy aren't worth the effort?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s been… about eight months or so.
